# BACKLOG.md — OTV5 — v5.12

**This tree's backlog.** Same rules as `WORKING_AGREEMENT.md` §18: ships in every
archive, one row per item, a title bump and a PART 3 entry per delivery. States (the
LAST cell, which `tests/check_ledger_parity.py` reads): 🔲 OPEN · ◐ built/pushed, not
yet proven on a box · ✅ closed · ⬛ dead. Revisions of THIS tree are cited bare
inside this repo (`r3`) and by prefix outside it (`OTV5 r3`); revisions of any other
repository are always prefixed (`OTV4TEST r316`, `dtp r489`) — a bare number here is
a row in `docs/GENESIS.md`, which starts at r0.

**PART 1 carries the open and unproven rows the seed inherited, verbatim, with their
ids and their citations prefixed; nothing below PART 1 predates r0.** A row closed in
place stays in place (the ledger's per-revision idiom); new closures go to PART 2.

**BUILT ≠ PUSHED ≠ BAKED** — the third means running on a box with the operator
watching the plan ledger daily.

---

| **AGT.4** | 🔲 **CROSS-SESSION MESSAGES NEVER WAIT ON AN APPROVAL DIALOG.** The operator, 2026-10-04: 'Backlog it.' Claude Code's docs (cross-session-messaging): a receiving session in a different permission class than the sender (bypass vs prompting) HOLDS an incoming message for its user's approval and drops it after dialogExpiry (5 min); there is no read receipt across machines. The control: crossSessionInbound = accept / hold / refuse, global (no per-peer allow). PROPOSED: "crossSessionInbound": "accept" in all three agents' user settings (QQQ-TEST, SPX-TEST, 1-REPORTER), and the three in one permission class - the operator's settings change. Until then: every message carries an id and an ACK; an unacked id is the only 'not read' signal. | 🔲 OPEN |

It NEVER anchors WHERE the limit sits. That separation is the whole point."* A target is a TRIGGER; putting it in the price is smuggling a trigger into an anchor. 🔑 **PARITY HAS NO CEILING** — it is `spot − strike`, so a 20-point move is a 20.00 limit that re-prices upward every tick and can only be lifted at fair value. This is **not a take-profit; it is a fair-value exit**: delta decides WHEN, parity decides AT WHAT PRICE. Below par delta the order does not rest at all, because parity is not fair value while real extrinsic remains. `limit_at_mark(mark, floor=...)` ALREADY DOES `px = max(px, floor)` — the floor parameter exists and no caller passes it. Every input is in hand: spot from `df_1m`, strike on the record, `current_delta` stashed by `position_manager`. ⚠️ **THE RISK IS ONE-SIDED, WHICH IS RARE ENOUGH TO STATE:** if the resting order fills you keep parity less a tick; if it does not, 15:45 crosses **exactly as it does today**. Before 15:50 it watches SILENTLY. 15:50-16:00, unhealthy two consecutive minutes with a position: SIGKILL then stop (the SIGTERM handler's STOPPED page would be a second message; a bot that will not die gets nothing sent beside it and ONE close-by-hand page), live: cancel the instrument's working orders, run the bot's own `flatten_all` every 15s to flat or 16:00, then ONE page naming each close and its P&L. The Telegram pair is taken OUT of the watchdog's process so the bot's code there sends no exit alerts; the bot is NOT restarted (no startup page) - it starts at the next boot. Once a day. 🔑 WHY A HEARTBEAT: bot.log went silent 703-1208s at 15:40 ET on 09-18/21/23, the window it acts in. Gate `tests/check_emergency_watchdog.py` W0-W18 (W18: stuck all day = exactly one page); 17 mutants killed. ⚠️ NOT COVERED: the whole box down; half days (unmodelled tree-wide). A deliberate stop with a position open at 15:50 is closed like a hang. OWED: restart the bot (heartbeat) THEN install the timer; `--status` once (env, Telegram, health). | ◐ BUILT |

On this box's own ledger `QQQ:PDH (R1):717.52` logged **399 touches in a 390-bar session**, and **566 of 607 levels (93%) logged zero** — the predecessor's AMZN-389 and its 68%/19% split, reproduced on QQQ. **(2) `ACCEPT_CLOSES = 2` COUNTED TICKS.** The same 5m close was re-read every tick, so the SECOND TICK always satisfied it — **~15 seconds and ONE close, not the two the constant names.** **(3) THE RUN NEVER RESET ON AN INSIDE CLOSE.** Only a *touch* (within `TOUCH_TOL_PCT`) cleared `beyond`, so two excursions **90 minutes apart**, with price six points inside for an hour in between, read as two consecutive closes and **retired the level** — pulling it off the board OTV4TEST r15 had only just repaired. ⚠️ **THIS WAS NEVER COSMETIC:** the runaway arms on ACCEPTED (§30) and the TCS triggers on it (§34), so two of the six trades were firing on a word that did not mean what it said. **OPERATOR'S RULINGS, 2026-09-13:** a touch is one per closed bar; two 5m bars is *"Hell no — 10 minutes leaves us nothing actionable, the move is already long over"*; a close back inside breaks the run. Built: the accounting is gated on the **CLOSED 1m bar** — the same bar the rejection fact already used, so the engine is on one clock — making `ACCEPT_CLOSES = 2` mean **two minutes**, deterministic rather than accidental. `check_level_rejection` v1.4 R1/R1b/R2/R2b/R2c/R3/R3b, **seven born red at OTV4TEST r17**, with R1b and R3b as positive controls green on both sides. | ✅ OTV4TEST r18 |

`_sources()` admitted them into a book of HORIZONTAL levels, where `_lid` bakes the price into the id — so **one moving rail became a new ledger row every time it drifted a cent.** **Measured here: 22 simultaneously-live `1h upper tine` rows spanning 5.79 points**, none retired, including four frozen positions of a fork that had already died and been superseded (`726.11 → 721.27` is the rebirth, visible in the data). ⚠️ **OTV4TEST r15 THOUGHT IT HAD FIXED THIS.** It removed `_tines()` from `_sources()` and pinned it with L11, *"no `fork1h/*` row in the ledger"* — but the mapper names its rails `1h upper tine`, so `_is_tine()` returned False and they came in through the **pool** door. **The canary was scoped to the name that had been removed, not the one that remained** (§20, one level up). 🔴 **AND LVL.1 WAS MASKING IT:** those rows carry `kind='high'`, which the emitter skips, so there are zero `level_event` rows on any tine provenance — **OTV4TEST r15 would have unmasked all of it on Monday**, turning ghost rails into visible board entries and live rejection facts. **FIXED to the operator's four clauses:** a moving pool never enters the ledger; `_fork_key()` (the fork's p0/p1/p2 anchors) is the projection's identity; a change of anchors drops every tine pierce state, where OTV4TEST r15's name-based drop could never fire because a reborn fork republishes the same three names; and `tines_now(price, minutes_back)` reads the rail **where it stood**, with the emitter reading one bar back because the extreme it judges is on the last CLOSED bar. `check_level_rejection` v1.5 F1/F1b/F2/F2b/F3/F3b/F4/F4b — F1 and F2 born red at OTV4TEST r18, F3 onward aborting there with `TypeError: tines_now() got an unexpected keyword argument 'minutes_back'`; F1b/F2b/F3b/F4 are controls green on both sides. | ✅ OTV4TEST r19 |

(2) A test of the **NEAR** edge registers the touch. (3) Acceptance beyond the **FAR** edge is the breach, and **retires the ENTIRE cluster, every member with it** — not only the edge accepted through. (4) Acceptance beyond the near edge alone declares **nothing**. (5) Every member **between** the edges is **SILENT**: no WICKED, no REJECTED, no ACCEPTED, no touch count. They are the zone's biography, not its voice. (6) ⚠️ **IT IS A GROUPING, NOT A NEW STORE AND NOT A NEW MAP.** The ledger goes on recording session extremes exactly as it does now; the ONE board (OTV4TEST r33) decides which two are the live edges and which are absorbed. 🔑 **AND IT DISSOLVES A PROBLEM RATHER THAN CREATING ONE:** `touch_count` does not have to migrate from the member to the zone, because under rule 5 the members never counted anything. **THE WIDTH IS A PRIOR AND §31 GOVERNS — IT IS NOT YET SET.** His framing: *"an area defined by the region several 1-day wicks occupy in a concurrent time frame — measured by extremes, but often characterized by the presence of wicks."* Measured locally on **28** QQQ daily bars (a mechanism, not a conclusion — §12): upper wick median 1.69 / p75 2.81, lower 2.12 / 2.71, **all wicks median 1.91, p75 2.71, p90 3.79** — 0.27% / 0.38% / 0.53% of spot, against a median daily range of 6.26 (a typical wick ≈ **30% of the day's range**). 🔑 **THE ANSWER LOOKS ROBUST RATHER THAN FITTED**, because the live gaps are **bimodal**: six gaps at or under 0.47 and four at or above 1.16, with **nothing in between**. Median and p75 therefore give the IDENTICAL grouping — **5 zones from 11 levels, two above spot and three below**; only p90 (3.79) over-merges, swallowing 720.06 and 716.75 into one 3.31-wide zone, which are two different trades. **OWED BEFORE IT BECOMES A NUMBER:** the same calculation on the S3 daily history (LVL.12's pull — one job, both answers) across more than one symbol; expressed as a **fraction of daily range or ATR rather than a fixed dollar amount**, or it breaks on the first volatility change and on every symbol but QQQ; and a **born-red gate on the asymmetry** — near edge counts the touch, far edge takes the breach and takes the whole cluster with it, interior declares nothing. **§36 CATEGORY: FOUNDATIONAL** — it changes what a level IS, which is the class §38.3 reserves to the operator. **SEQUENCED BEHIND LVL.12 by his ruling: "wait till the history is in."** ✅ **CLOSED OTV4TEST r39 — ALL THREE OWED ITEMS PAID.** (a) **More than one symbol, and the answer changed shape because of it:** the width is NOT a fraction of spot and NOT a fixed dollar amount — it is the **median larger-side HOURLY wick, recomputed every board**. Measured across QQQ, MU and SPX, the wick holds at **31-37% of the median hourly range** while the same wick **as a percent of spot spans four-fold**. A stored percentage would have been wrong on MU by roughly 4x. Operator: *"the width ruling is sound as a one size fits most."* ⚠️ **AND THE SPX HALF OF THAT SAMPLE IS THINNER THAN I FIRST PRESENTED** — see PRE.5: the warehouse's hourly series for SPX is partial since 2026-08-21, so SPX replicates on July–August backfills only. QQQ and MU are clean. (b) **Expressed as a measurement, not a constant** — `level_map.zone_width()`, nothing in the file is a number. (c) **The born-red gate on the asymmetry:** `tests/check_zones.py` Z1–Z7, **born red 13 of 15 at OTV4TEST r38**. 🔴 **AND BUILDING IT FOUND A DEFECT THE SPEC COULD NOT HAVE PREDICTED:** `board()` ran OTV4TEST r29's `_walked()` **before** any grouping, which is correct for single levels and destructive for clusters — measured on the live ledger, **43 rows became 21 and the six levels stacked on spot (716.75 … 718.04) became ONE** before a zone could form. Cluster first, walk second; `_walked` is gone from `board()` and Z7 pins it. 🔑 **A SEVENTH RULE ARRIVED DURING THE BUILD:** *"'one zone containing spot' isn't a zone, then. It's done — as a zone is defined, it's finished. There's no reclaim trade for that."* A zone straddling price retires TRAVERSED — on a **closed bar**, never a tick, because OTV4TEST r5 ruled a wick never spends a level and retirement has no undo. | ✅ OTV4TEST r39 |
| **LVL.11** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r30) **OTV4TEST r29 SHIPPED TWO DEFECTS, FOUND READING ITS OWN NOON BAKE.** The bake itself was clean — restart 12:00:37 ET on `0c3b01b`, first reconcile `129 row(s) retired, 9 held`, the walk exactly the map (up 715.42 / 717.68 / 719.70 / 720.06, down 701.16), no level event written by history. **(1) A CONFIRMED LEGACY ROW KEPT `timeframe='session'`:** the reconcile stamped `created_ts` but not the timeframe, which `upsert_level` never rewrites, and the book-beyond-the-tape rule matches only `session:%` — so when Saturday's purge trims the 1m tape past 09-09, the HELD 720.06 / 719.70 / 717.68 would have retired `NOT_A_LEVEL`. **(2) THE OTV4TEST r29 CHANGELOG SAID STALE VWAP IDS RETIRE; THE CODE SKIPPED EVERY `dynamic` ROW** — 515 live after the bake. INSIDE also trades every session (22) where A1 fires 11–18 times, because the break it waits for often never comes — and when it does, the break has already spent the runway. | 🔲 OPEN |

The +39.8R in underlying points is −$876 in the option. **Wins mean +17.5%, losses mean −51.8%** — theta inverts the asymmetry. ⚠️ **BUT THE MODEL IS NOT THE STRATEGY:** I used a synthetic 0.5× OR stop; §37 specifies *thesis dead = a close back through the ENTRY boundary · fizzle · 15:45 · **no premium stop***, which for the INSIDE variant exits on the first 1m close back through the range boundary — minutes, not hours. I envision every trade to be 'right' in its own time, as long as our entries are weighing the right information… two separate trades, even potentially trading opposite directions, may have been right at the time that they fired… If we write our plans properly and we write our strategies properly, a given set of conditions for entry may be right at a particular time and I don't see any reason to stop it from firing if it cleared the bar, even if the thesis is the opposite of an existing open trade."* 🔑 **THE COLLISION BECOMES UNREACHABLE RATHER THAN ADJUDICATED.** With `blocked_by` empty permanently, a grant can never contradict a block, so none of the three candidate rulings below needs taking. **OTV4TEST r35's named sets stay in place, config-toggleable and simply never armed** — which is what a named-set design is for, and why this reversal costs nothing. 🔑 **AND THE GRANT CHANGES MEANING, FOR THE BETTER.** Under hierarchy a grant was PERMISSION — an override of a block. With nothing to override it becomes **INFORMATION**: *I evaluated this break and concluded it is a harvest; the fade is yours if you want it.* The receiver still clears its own bar. That is strictly more useful than an override and is closer to what BRK.1 wants from Breakout→hunt (OTV4TEST r48). ⚠️ **WHAT DOES NOT DISAPPEAR — IT MOVES FROM PERMISSION TO CAPITAL.** His argument is about CORRECTNESS and it holds: two setups can both be right at the moment they fire. But the constraint hierarchy was accidentally supplying was **EXPOSURE**, not correctness. Four strategies at full risk-per-trade is 4x one, and OTV4TEST r44 gave the ORB a full-budget slice where it previously took 3%. **The catastrophic cap is now the ONLY thing between 'everything cleared its bar' and a very large day** — which is answerable with a number rather than a hierarchy, and is his to set. 🔑 **AND IT IS SMALLER THAN THE ARITHMETIC SUGGESTS:** two opposite-direction trades on one underlying are partly a HEDGE — gross risk 2x, net directional exposure much less. ⚠️ **WHAT STILL NEEDS COORDINATING, AND IS NOT HIERARCHY: `max_open_of_type`** (sweep 2, everything else 1). That is not one trade gating another, it is a strategy declining to stack on ITSELF, which his reasoning does not cover and which stays. **THE ORIGINAL QUESTION, KEPT FOR THE RECORD:** Operator, 2026-09-18: *"it's gonna be non-competing for the next couple of weeks, but after that we will be going back to a hierarchy based and there will be some blocking between the trades."* 🔑 **THE TABLE IS ALREADY BUILT FOR THAT HALF** — OTV4TEST r35 made `blocks` and `blocked_by` NAMED SETS, empty by his ruling, overlaid from `config.ADMISSION_RULES`, so re-introducing blocking is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. That was the whole point of the design. ⚠️ **WHAT IS NOT SETTLED IS THE COLLISION.** `execution/handoff.py` was written when the slot rule was binary: a live grant made *"the slot rule yield for that strategy only"*, and **the slot rule no longer exists** (OTV4TEST r43). When `blocked_by` comes back, a grant and a block can name the same pair and DISAGREE — and that is not hypothetical, because BRK.1 has Breakout DEFERRING TO THE HUNT BY GRANT (OTV4TEST r48) while hierarchy would plausibly have one block the other. **Three candidate rulings, all defensible, and it is the operator's call: (a) the grant WINS — an explicit token from the blocking strategy is consent, and blocking is only the default; (b) the block WINS — a grant answers the slot question only, which is what its own docstring says, and the slot question is gone; (c) the grant wins ONLY when `from_strategy` is the blocker itself**, so A-blocks-B plus A-grants-to-B admits B, while C's grant cannot override A's block. **(c) is the narrowest and matches the grant's original intent most closely.** ⚠️ **DECIDE IT WHILE IT IS CHEAP.** Today both mechanisms are inert — blocks are empty and the only grant path is hunt→sweep — so the collision is unreachable and costs nothing to settle. On the first hierarchy session it becomes a live ordering question with money on it, and the failure mode is silent: the deferred-to strategy simply never fires and nothing says why. **OWED: a born-red checker covering all three pairs (grant only, block only, both) whichever ruling is taken.** | ✅ OTV4TEST r50 |
| **ADM.1** | ✅ **(OTV4TEST r43) THE OPEN-BRANCH BLOCK IS THE LAST OF OTV4TEST r35's SEVEN MECHANISMS STILL DECIDING WHAT MAY OPEN.** 🔑 **OPERATOR'S RULING 2026-09-18, WHICH SETTLES IT AND WIDENS IT:** *"I want the orb, hunt, breakout & sweep all able to fire & non-competing. We only have one session at a time so we need to maximize every opportunity on these setups. In live trading, we will revert back to hierarchy-based."* ⚠️ **THE POSTURE IS TEST-BOX-ONLY AND THAT IS THE POINT** — non-compete exists to harvest the maximum number of comparable setups from ONE session, not because hierarchy is wrong. The fleet reverts to hierarchy, and because the whole table overlays from `config.ADMISSION_RULES` (OTV4TEST r35), that reversion is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. 🔴 **AND IT IS BIGGER THAN REMOVING A GATE: `attempt_new_entry`'s `signal` CASCADE *IS* THE COMPETITION** — one variable, five `if signal is None` guards, winner-take-all, so the first strategy to fire consumes the tick. Non-competing means each ADMITTED strategy is asked and executed independently. 🔑 **THE PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS AND IS PROVEN IN PRODUCTION:** `_attempt_hunt` (OTV4TEST r12) asks its own plan and executes ADDITIVE, taking no slot — *"the hunt runs beside the ORB, never in its slot"*. The conversion is to that template, not to something new. ⚠️ **AND IT DELETES HAND-ROLLED CAPS:** `_attempt_hunt` carries its own *"a hunt is already open on this box"* check, which is `max_open_of_type: 1` doing the same job twice — two mechanisms for one rule is how they drift. ⚠️ **CONCURRENCY CEILING RISES:** worst case four directional debits in one session at full risk-per-trade, with sweep's cap at 2, and Breakout makes five entry types. | `main.py`'s tick loop still reads `if not pos_mgr.has_blocking_position(): attempt_new_entry(...)`, so a box holding an ORB or runaway debit refuses every credit entry — **against the operator's ruling of 2026-09-17**: *"to the greatest extent possible I do not want to set a maximum number of open positions… I WANT sweep to be able to fire while we have an active runaway in progress. We will rely on the runaway to exit gracefully using ITS OWN stops."* The admission table's per-type caps (sweep 2, everything else 1) are built to replace it and `blocks`/`blocked_by` are named sets, empty by his ruling. 🔴 **WHAT BLOCKS THE DELETION, MEASURED AT OTV4TEST r40 RATHER THAN GUESSED:** removing the gate orphans `_attempt_butterfly` and `_attempt_sweep_on_grant`, whose only callers are the three direct calls in that same branch — and those are **not** duplicates of `attempt_new_entry`'s own dispatch. `_attempt_butterfly(additive=True)` is OTV4TEST r161's EXEMPT path, asked every tick of its slot with a fire APPENDED to whatever is open; `attempt_new_entry`'s butterfly sits behind `elif signal is None:` and competes for the slot. Running both asks three strategies TWICE A TICK; deleting the three loses the exemption that cost MU, NFLX and TSLA a full credit session on 2026-08-31 (OTV4TEST r197). ⚠️ **THE `additive` ARGUMENT IS ALREADY A NO-OP IN THAT BRANCH** — OTV4TEST r197 made it a fact (`if additive or _pm.has_open_position()`) — so the de-duplication is tractable; it is the ASK-ORDERING that needs deciding, not the record-keeping. **§36 CATEGORY: FOUNDATIONAL** — it changes what may be open simultaneously, which §38.3 reserves to the operator. **NOT ATTEMPTED UNATTENDED:** found at 23:05 on a box that wakes into a trading session at 08:00. | ✅ OTV4TEST r43 |
| **PRE.6** | (id was PRE.5 until the 2026-10-03 cleanup - it named two rows) 🔲 **THE WAREHOUSE'S CANDLE SERIES ABOVE 1m ARE SYSTEMATICALLY PARTIAL — A MAINLINE DEFECT, FOR THE CONTROL AGENT, NOT THIS BOX.** Found 2026-09-18 while measuring the zone width on SPX and MU. **`warehouse/s3_push.py:886`**: `SELECT * FROM candles WHERE symbol=? AND interval=? AND ts_epoch_ms > ?` — `push_candles` advances a **high-water mark on the BAR'S OWN TIMESTAMP**. Once a bar is pushed its stamp sits at or below the watermark **forever**, so every later `UPDATE` to that same row — which is what a 1h bar *is* for the 59 minutes after its first tick — **is never pushed again.** Whichever state the pusher happened to catch is what the warehouse keeps. ⚠️ **CORRECT FOR 1m, WRONG FOR EVERY LONGER INTERVAL:** a 1m bar is written once; 5m, 15m, 1h and 1d are mutable rows. 🔑 **AND THE SAME FILE ALREADY HAS THE RIGHT PATTERN** — `push_trades` at `:613` pushes *"every trade row whose CONTENT has changed since last push"*, content-based CDC. `push_candles` uses a timestamp watermark, and for a mutable row those are not the same thing. **MEASURED, every 1h object in `raw/candles` across 31 partitions:** SPX has **ZERO complete bars since 2026-08-21** — median push lag **−59.5 minutes**, i.e. every bar pushed ~30s after its hour OPENED and never again, four weeks of opening ticks; QQQ is partial but survivable at 11–16 complete of 28–44 rows per bar-date, so its coverage is thin rather than absent. ⚠️ **MEASURED-ON-ONE-BOX-ONLY, AND THE CONTROL AGENT'S INDEPENDENT READ DISAGREES (OTV4TEST r65).** It measured **QQQ 0 of 21 complete** on 2026-09-16/17/18 — every symbol it sampled fully frozen, not SPX-shaped at all. Ours may have come from an earlier window. **The discrepancy was deliberately NOT bought**: both sides agreed it changes no conclusion — recoverability is settled per-interval by the fifteen-box fan-out and the 08-21 start date is confirmed independently on both sides — and a dedicated S3 run is the ~3,700 sequential `get_object` sweep §38.1 warns about. **The honest state of this figure is one box, one window, not a fleet property.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ABOUT SPX IS BROKEN** — its box pushes 192 objects a day, trades/EOD/plan_ledger all current through 09-17. The defect simply lands on its 16-rows-a-day cadence every time. ⚠️ **THE SILENCE IS THE COST:** any control-side report reading warehouse candles above 1m has been reading opening ticks some invisible fraction of the time, and it renders as a slightly-wrong number, never an error — the plausible-silence class this repo keeps finding in its own code. **OWED: a heads-up to the control agent with the evidence** (`s3_push.py:886`, the −59.5min SPX lag since 08-21, the `push_trades` contrast at `:613`). **NOT OURS TO FIX** — the brief is explicit that mainline is only touched for a significant flaw found here, and this is the "surface it" half, not the "change it" half. **Operator's ruling, 2026-09-18: address it on a weekend.** ⚠️ **AND IT NARROWS A CLAIM I MADE THE SAME NIGHT:** the zone-width replication on SPX rests on July–August backfills only, so the width is replicated on QQQ and MU cleanly and on SPX's older data — a smaller sample than I first presented. 🔑 **DISCHARGED 2026-09-19: DELIVERED TO THE CONTROL AGENT, AND IT CAME BACK STRONGER.** It verified the mechanism independently in otv4 r389 **by reading** (`s3_push.py:926` the hwm load, `:929` the bound SELECT, `:935`→`:945` the watermark advance) and found a better contrast than ours: **`push_trades:636`'s own docstring states the reasoning `push_candles` needed and did not get** — *"Trade rows MUTATE — written at entry, rewritten at exit"* — one function above the one that needed it. **That is what moves this from a design trade-off to a defect.** 🔑 **THEIR SCOPE NOTE, CREDITED, AND IT RESCUED ONE OF OUR OWN RESULTS:** `raw/candles` and `raw/ohlc` are **different pushers** — the file-based one at `:871` keys its ledger on a **content sha**, so the `ohlc` prefix is **UNAFFECTED**. **CONSEQUENCE: OTV4TEST r47 STANDS.** Our volume-expansion study (n=736, back to 2026-07-08) read `ohlc`, and it is load-bearing — it is the measurement that ruled volume expansion does not separate a breakout, and therefore why the Breakout's gates are a router rather than a filter. Had it been built on watermark-frozen bars the whole gate design reopened. **It was not, and we would not have known to check without their paragraph.** 🔴 **THE REPAIR IS NOT FORWARD-ONLY, AND THERE IS A DEADLINE.** Their first reading was that a CDC fix could not heal the frozen bars because the store is a pruned rolling window (`push_candles`' own docstring). **Measured here instead** — `RETENTION_DAYS` at `warehouse/retention_purge.py:172`, against `feed_store.db` depth on 2026-09-19: `1m` floor 09-14 (policy 5d, exact) · `5m` floor **09-09** (policy 10d, exact) · `15m` floor **09-02** (policy 20d, **SHALLOWER**) · `1h` floor **07-21** (policy 60d, exact) · `1d` floor **08-10** (never purged). **1h reaches a month BEFORE the 08-21 corruption, so the rows that would supply the correct values were never pruned** — a CDC fix or a one-time watermark reset above 1m heals the existing range, not merely future bars. **CLOCK: the 08-21 bars age out of the 60-day window around 2026-10-20**, rolling forward daily after that. 5m (08-21→09-09) and 15m (08-21→09-02) are **already partly gone**. 🔑 **TWO GENERAL RULES THIS PRODUCED, AND THEY OUTLIVE THE DEFECT.** (1) **`NEVER_PURGE` is a statement about the purge, not about the data** — our `1d` series has no purge clock and still has a floor, at 08-10, because that is when collection began; on a box whose daily collection started after 08-21 there is nothing to recover and the policy would never say so. (2) **The policy is a ceiling on depth, not a promise of it** — `15m` measured three days shallower than its own window. **Every row of a recoverability table must be measured per box per interval or it is an upper bound wearing a fact's clothes.** ⚠️ **THEIR PARTIAL-REPAIR WARNING, WHICH IS THE BEST POINT EITHER SIDE MADE:** a watermark reset on `5m` would re-push correct rows for the last ten days and **nothing** for 08-21→09-09, leaving the bucket holding a mix of healed and frozen bars **with nothing distinguishing them** — worse than uniformly bad data, because it destroys the one property telling a reader which rows to distrust. **And the marking must be computed BY the repair, not appended after it**: an operation that knows which rows it could not heal already holds everything the marker needs, and a second pass is the thing that does not happen. **The marker is per symbol per interval**, carrying that series' own floor. ⚠️ **CONSTRAINT ON OUR OWN RESEARCH SURFACE, recorded because it binds every future study:** anything we read from `raw/candles` **above 1m** over the affected window is reading frozen opening ticks. `raw/candles/interval=1m` is safe; `raw/ohlc` is safe; everything else above 1m carries the caveat until the bucket is repaired or marked. **We do not push, so we contribute nothing to the corruption — we only read it**, which is the asymmetry that explains why this fork found it and mainline did not. **STILL OWED TO US:** their fifteen-box `SELECT interval, MIN(ts_epoch_ms), COUNT(*) FROM candles GROUP BY interval;` fan-out, **read 1d-floor-first** — a 1h gap announces itself as a deadline, a 1d floor after 08-21 announces nothing at all. Blocked on their side by a dead Bash tool, not by a decision. ◐ heads-up DELIVERED 2026-09-19 — the repair is mainline's | 🔲 OPEN |

### The generated maps, and what the box actually runs

| id | item | state |

| **MAP.1** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r18) **THE WRITE MAP WAS BLIND TO A TABLE NAMED THROUGH A CONSTANT, AND CALLED FOUR MORE "(unattributed)".** `derived/base.py` writes `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {STATUS_TABLE}`; every pattern in `gen_write_map.py` requires a literal, so **`derived_engine_status` — written by every derived engine on every tick, read by `tools/manifold_health.py:246` — appeared nowhere**, while the flag list reported *"No writer (0): none"* and the header reported 29 tables. The document whose stated job is to make an unwritten table visible was blind to a writer with no literal, and it **rendered as a smaller plausible number rather than an error** — the defect shape §35 names. Also resolved, all four verified against the live files on the box: `gate_disposition`/`plan_check`/`plan_tick` → `derived_store.db`, `resting_orders` → `resting_orders.db`, **a fourth database the map had never named**. 30 tables now, no `(unattributed)` section. ⚠️ `--check` was rc=0 the whole time: **current and accurate are different questions**, and only the first one is gated. | ✅ OTV4TEST r18 |
| **CFG.1** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r18) **THE LIVE PIERCE FLOOR CAME FROM A `getattr` DEFAULT WITH AN ORPHAN BESIDE IT.** `derived/levels.py` read `getattr(_cfg, "SWEEP_CS_MAX_REJECTION_PCT", 0.0025)` and **that key was not defined in `config.py` at all**, so every pierce band on the box ran on the literal — while `SWEEP_MIN_REJECTION_PCT = 0.003` sat in config with **zero readers**. The predecessor found this same shape at **15x**; ours is 1.20x, which is exactly why nobody saw it. Fixed by DEFINING the key at the value already running (no behaviour change, the knob becomes real) and labelling the orphan in place so the next reader does not tune the dead one. `LEVEL_ACCEPT_CLOSES` added the same way, so LVL.4's acceptance count moves without a redeploy. ⚠️ **THE CLASS IS NOT CLOSED:** `SWEEP_CS_REJECTION_FRESH_BARS` is read by `strategy/sweep_plan.py` and is likewise undefined in config, so SWP.1's "config-overridable" is only true if you know to add a key that is not there. Sweep it properly — every `getattr(_cfg, ...)` in the tree — as its own row. | ✅ OTV4TEST r18 |
| **LAND.1** | ⬜ **A GENUINE RE-ISSUE CANNOT PASS THE LAND GATE.** §26 mandates that re-issuing a revision that ALREADY SHIPPED suffixes it — `r185_r2`, `r185_r3`. `tools/check_land_discipline.py` finds the ledger row with `^\|\s*\*\*(r\d+[a-z]?)\*\*\s*\|`, which matches `OTV4TEST r18` and `r18a` and **cannot match `r18_r2`**. So the gate that enforces §35 refuses the exact revision id §26 requires: the lander appends the row, the checker reports *"no row for r18_r2 — a revision absent from the ledger did not happen"*, and the land dies with the payload loose in the tree. **Hit for real on 2026-09-13** while landing this revision. ⚠️ **IT FAILS CLOSED, WHICH IS THE SAFE DIRECTION** — and that is why nobody has found it: the `_rN` path has simply never been exercised, so a rule this repo has carried since 2026-08-19 has never once been landable. Not urgent here, because this revision was a re-cut of an UNLANDED archive and therefore keeps its own number (the OTV4TEST r12 precedent) — but a real post-land correction will hit it head-on. One character in the regex; the fix needs a born-red fixture with an `_r2` row. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **HYG.10** | ⬜ **A CHECK RAN THE REAL RETENTION PURGE AS A SIDE EFFECT — A HYGIENE DEFECT, NOT DATA LOSS.** `check_audit_20260823` A1 drove `self_close.main()` with only `subprocess.run` faked, so self_close went on to call `retention_purge.main(["--apply"])` for real on the box's live `data/feed_store.db` (which `purge()` opens regardless of OT_DERIVED_DB), on each full check sweep — OTV4TEST r20's and the OTV4TEST r25/r26 sweeps of 2026-09-13/14. **Corrected by the operator's ruling (2026-09-14):** the purge is *"safe to run anytime any day"* — it keeps the windows the tenors need and never touches trades — *"otherwise it did what it's supposed to do."* So the rows it removed (Thursday 09-10's option prints and quotes, past the 3-day window) are the policy working, not an incident; I first reported them as a loss, which overstated it. | **BOX.12** | ✅ (OTV4TEST r70) **/tmp IS tmpfs — IT IS RAM — AND SESSION SCRATCHPADS ACCUMULATE UNTIL EVERY SHELL DIES SILENTLY.** Measured: `/tmp` here is a **455 MB tmpfs of 908 MB physical**, and the agent scratchpad is keyed by SESSION ID, so every new session mints a directory and nothing removes the old ones. One of mine reached **108 MB in three git clones** — RAM the bot could not have, and part of why a corpus study was OOM-reaped. 🔴 **WHAT IT COSTS IS THE SHELL, NOT DISK.** When tmpfs fills, every Bash spawn needs a temp file for its shell snapshot and dies BEFORE executing: instant exit 1, no stdout, no stderr, while Read/Write keep working because they never spawn. **The control box hit exactly this — 1.5 GB across 57,304 files, `df` reading 384 MB free throughout because the binding limit was a `usrquota`, not free space.** An agent that looks brain-dead rather than out of space, with nothing logged anywhere (§0.5). ⚠️ **THIS BOX WAS PROTECTED BY ACCIDENT, NOT DESIGN** — tmpfs is volatile and the midnight halt plus the 08:00 wake clears it daily. Control does not reboot daily. **BUILT:** `_claude_purge_scratch` in devtools (HAND OFF, RESUME and the picker) and `purge_scratch()` in `tools/claude_boot.py`. **ORDER IS THE OPERATOR'S RULING — PURGE, THEN KILL, THEN LAUNCH** — not cosmetic: if tmpfs is already full the tmux kill may not spawn either. 🔑 **IT ARCHIVES, IT NEVER DELETES** (`~/claude_scratch_archive`, 14-day sweep the only deleting path) — the mainline control agent's finding, from a near-miss where a deleting purge would have destroyed an entire unlanded revision sitting in a handed-off session's scratchpad. **The harness itself instructs every agent to work in the scratchpad**, so treating it as disposable is wrong by construction. 🔴 **AND THEIR REVIEW CAUGHT A LIVE DEFECT IN MY FIRST CUT:** the purge ran UNCONDITIONALLY while `bring_up()` returns "already running" WITHOUT killing anything — and under systemd `CLAUDE_SCRATCH` is unset, so nothing was protected. **`systemctl start optbot-claude-boot.service` against a LIVE session would have archived the running agent's working directory out from under it**, and the operator ran exactly that command on 2026-09-20. Gated on `agent_alive()` now: **only reclaim when you are replacing.** Free-tmpfs rides the boot status string, so it reaches the boot Telegram beside the IP with no change to `alert_manager`. GATE `tests/check_claude_purge.py` P0–P10, **born red 10 of 11**; P7 control green both sides. | ✅ CLOSED |

### The operator's tooling

| id | item | state |
|---|---|---|
| **MBX.1** | 🔲 **A SHARED S3 MAILBOX BETWEEN THIS FORK AND THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT — DESIGNED 2026-09-19, NOT APPROVED, NOT BUILT, NOTHING EXISTS.** Proposed at the **mainline operator's instruction**; it needs **both** operators' sign-off and an IAM change, and neither agent requests the grant for the other. **SHAPE:** prefix `mailbox/` — deliberately **outside `raw/`**, which has a standing never-delete rule and must not acquire a second purpose. Layout `mailbox/to=<recipient>/from=<sender>/<sent_utc>-<msg_id>.json`, acks at `mailbox/ack/to=<original_sender>/<msg_id>.json` so each side lists exactly ONE prefix for its own receipts. Envelope mirrors the warehouse `_wrap` idiom plus `in_reply_to`. **The mailbox is TRANSPORT, not an archive** — 90-day lifecycle, and **anything that matters is landed into the RECEIVING side's own ledger by the receiving side**, because the record must outlive the lifecycle. 🔑 **OUR OBJECTIONS, ALL ACCEPTED BY THE OTHER SIDE.** (1) 🔴 **IT SOLVES DURABILITY AND AUDIT BUT NOT LATENCY, AND LATENCY IS WHERE 2026-09-19's ENTIRE VALUE CAME FROM** — a defect was surfaced and verified across four files in twenty minutes, and a wrong inference corrected in one round; through a polled mailbox both are days apart or never, against a clock expiring 10-20. **Nothing polls a mailbox**, and §38.7 says plainly that an assistant does not run continuously. So a **session-start LIST of one's own inbox prefix is a REQUIREMENT**, in the handoff reading order beside the transcript read — one LIST, no timer, no grant beyond the read. (2) **Both channels, different jobs** — the live session route for work where latency IS the value, the mailbox for durable handoff and session-death survival; a replacement would trade the thing that worked for the things that failed. (3) **`provenance` mandatory, and PER-CLAIM** — `measured` / `read` / `reported` / `inferred`, with the envelope pinned to the **weakest value present** as a backstop. The day produced two retractions and both were **an inference sent in the register of a measurement**; and `reported` is ours, because a third-party fact relayed onward is none of the other three and unlabelled hearsay is how a number launders itself into a permanent record. (4) **IAM tightenings:** Put scoped to the **other** side's inbox only (neither side can write its own inbox or overwrite the other's sent objects, so forged inbound is impossible), **no `DeleteObject` for either agent at all** (an agent that can delete can erase the audit trail), and an **explicit Deny on `s3:PutObject` for `raw/*`**, because our read grant is bucket-wide and an Allow scoped to `mailbox/*` is worth only as much as the absence of a second Allow nobody has audited. 🔴 **WHAT IT WOULD COST US, AND IT IS THE OPERATOR'S RULING AND NOT THE ASSISTANT'S.** It needs `s3:PutObject` on `vertigo-warehouse-tx9ai`. **Our OTV4TEST r31 grant is READ-ONLY and that is recorded as a SAFETY RULING, not a permission detail** (§38.3): this box does not push **because another QQQ box already does**, and a second pusher into `raw/.../sym=QQQ/` would not error — latest-wins by `pushed_at_utc` would silently overwrite the live box's rows in every fleet report. ⚠️ **That reason does not apply to a `mailbox/` prefix — there is no collision there — but the ruling as written is broader than the reason that produced it, and narrowing a safety ruling is the operator's call.** Nothing here implies unmasking `s3-push.service`, and the assistant would object if it did. **If the operator declines, the existing session channel plus the ACK convention is a working answer and the other side has been told so in those terms.** ◐ OTV4TEST r65 — **OUR OPERATOR HAS RULED: WANTED, DEFERRED.** 2026-09-19, verbatim: *"I want the mailbox, however this is working well for the moment. Perhaps during RTH we would tackle something like that."* **Not a decline and not a yes-now.** The live session channel proved sufficient across a full day of defect exchange, which is itself evidence for the original objection — the mailbox solves durability and audit, and latency is where the value turned out to be. Nothing built, nothing in the bucket, no IAM requested. His telemetry precondition stands unchanged whenever it is built. **Mainline's operator has not ruled; their agent has been told ours has, and that the ruling is "yes, later".** Revisit during RTH. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **PIN.1** | ◐ (OTV4TEST r127) **OTV4TEST r97's PIN-PROXIMITY GATE IS OFF PENDING DATA, AND IT IS SWITCHED FROM configure.sh ITEM 7.** Operator, 2026-09-24: *"Turn it off. It misleads whoever diagnoses from the log. A worktree sweep could not see it (the path is absolute, not repo-relative) - found by reading `_setup_logging` while building OTV4TEST r146. Fix, same shape as OTV4TEST r13/r109/r144: an `OT_LOG_FILE` override, set by boot_sweep and land.sh to each checker's scratch. | ◐ OTV4TEST r194 |
| **S3.15** | ◐ (OTV4TEST r147 BUILT; **SOFI's six entries cleared only after the bake, by 1-REPORTER on its operator's yes**) **A FAILED DRAIN HALTED AND PURGED INSTEAD OF HOLDING, AND AN S3 DELETE THAT EMPTIES A PREFIX NEVER HEALS.** `s3_push --verify`'s OTV4TEST r180 heal repairs a short prefix only when `got > 0`, so a prefix a deliberate delete EMPTIED (got=0) stays SHORT forever; with a gap <= 2 it prints COUNTER DRIFT (*'objects present'* — false, they are absent), and `self_close` let that drift line override `not ok` WHATEVER `failed=` said. MEASURED 2026-09-26 in a scratch sim of the real `main(--verify)` + self_close's parse: a failed drain with no short prefix HOLDS; the same failed drain plus one gap-1 orphan HALTS and runs the purge. SOFI carried SIX such prefixes (the 09-24/25 contamination deletes; 1-REPORTER's read-only dry verify: 6 at got=0, 4 partials 27/26, 29/28, 29/28, 2/1 that heal themselves); mainline carried 31 across 15 boxes (the 09-23 WH.20 refile), cleared by otv4 r436, SHORT 0 on all 15. The purge is bounded (measured, `purge(apply=True)` on scratch stores): one halt deletes nothing from that day - the shortest window is 3 days, series are push-mark clamped, candles and the chain_snapshots tree are age-only - so per event this is an ALARM defect, and a data-loss defect only across multi-day silent halts. **THE FIX, BOTH HALVES SHARED WITH otv4 (WA §38.2):** `warehouse/counter_pop.py` (otv4 r436 verbatim) removes ONLY ledger entries whose prefix LISTs zero objects, refuses partials and absent keys by name, takes the push lock (rc=3 without it), dry unless `--apply`; its gate `tests/check_counter_pop.py` v1.1 (otv4 r438 verbatim: N6 runs the WRITE path, which v1.0 never did - all four peer-run mutants were green on v1.0; N7 binds every name and call shape of the REAL s3_push, compiled from SOURCE because a stale good .pyc made the first N7 pass a same-size rename - reproduced here). `warehouse/self_close.py` v1.5 (2a): drift counts only when the DRAIN line reads `failed=0`; gate `tests/check_self_close_hold.py` H1-H5 + H4b, BORN RED on 0990101 at H4 and H4b only, three mutants killed. Operator 2026-09-26: fix 1 *'Concur'*, 2a approved, sequence *'Concur, and work closely with the peer'*. **NOT IN OTV4TEST r147, IN SEQUENCE:** 2b (an emptied prefix is reported EMPTIED, never drift) and ~~2c (heal only gaps <= 2)~~ **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-26 (operator: "Approve on all"), and WHY, so it is not proposed again:** it rested on s3_push's "a gap of 3+ STILL HOLDS THE BOX" comment, which PREDATES OTV4TEST r180 and was never updated — both agents read a stale comment as the specification. OTV4TEST r180 (2026-08-28, mainline GENESIS) heals any short prefix when the drain is clean (failed=0) and S3 holds objects (n>0), with NO gap bound, deliberately: the ledger counts PUTs while S3 counts KEYS, and re-pushes (timer, harvest, restart) inflate it past 2 as the steady state — the `<=2` in OTV4TEST r180's row is its PROBLEM STATEMENT ("the drift diagnosis only forgave gaps <=2 and two months of accumulation exceeded it"), not its remedy. Bounding the heal at 2 would have reinstated the nightly false holds OTV4TEST r180 ended ("it's actually costing me money"). Found by 1-REPORTER; verified on this tree (heal at s3_push :1309 has no gap condition). The stale comment is corrected by otv4 r444 (5800e81), MIRRORED here as OTV4TEST r150 (warehouse/s3_push.py v4.10, comment only — proven by an AST comparison with docstrings stripped, identical to HEAD, with a one-character code change as the must-fail control). ⚠️ THE RESIDUAL TRADE STANDS AND IS THE OPERATOR'S: on a clean drain a genuine partial loss is healed and logged, neither held nor alerted (the 10 -> 6 sim). 2b still waits for zero got=0 prefixes on every box. **BAKE:** this box does not run self_close; SOFI and AAL at a bake run from control. | ◐ OTV4TEST r147 |
| **LIVE.1** | ⬜ **CASH POSITIONS — THE PLAN, AND THE SIZING CATCH.** The operator, 2026-09-24: small live positions *"so that we can see what fills and exits actually look like under real market constraints"*, first at ~10% of the paper risk. MEASURED with the real sizer at 10% (risk $105, ramp $100/$1,000): Breakout/ORB/VOLT size 2-6 contracts (the curves compress); the runaway/hunt budget rule REFUSES a normal $1.90 contract; credit spreads fit only $1-wide ($2/$3/$5 wings refused); butterflies refused above ~$1.05 debit. His read: *"the number of contracts is going to be tough at 10%"*. 🔑 **HIS PROPOSAL, TO RULE AT THE ONE-MONTH REVIEW:** *"leaving the budget as is and blocking our three most unprofitable trades from participating"* — confirmed: live at the CURRENT budget ($1,050/trade; ramp $1,000 at risk to $10,000 deployed; $5,000 daily cap) *"and I would be even better with that if our losers don't get to dip their hands into that"*. Bottom three on 2026-09-24's book: SweepCreditSpread (11, -$1,053, 0% win), ATPButterfly (2, -$627), RunawayContinuation (29, -$105) — re-rank on the month, OTV4TEST r131 changes ORB/Breakout mechanics. ⚠️ Paper fills are at the MARK (`PAPER_FILL_SLIPPAGE_PCT` 0) — at ~18 trades/session and 25-60 contracts, a penny spread crossed both ways is ~$700/session against a simulated +$800 edge: a cost model for paper fills is owed before paper P&L is trusted at this size. | 🔲 OPEN — review ~2026-10-24 |
